# Fix cycles_and_basins to handle domains that don't include every image (e.g., T1 maps {1..16} into {0..15}).
from collections import defaultdict
import pandas as pd
import json, os
def cycles_and_basins_partial(T: dict):
domain = sorted(T.keys())
image_set = set(T.values())
# extend graph with images not in domain by treating them as terminal (no further mapping)
full_nodes = sorted(set(domain) | image_set)
T_ext = {x: T[x] if x in T else None for x in full_nodes}
cycles = []
basins = defaultdict(list)
for n in full_nodes:
visited = {}
seq = []
x = n
while x is not None and x not in visited:
visited[x] = len(seq)
seq.append(x)
x = T_ext.get(x, None)
cyc = tuple()
if x is not None and x in visited:
mu = visited[x]
cyc = tuple(seq[mu:])
if cyc:
rots = [tuple(cyc[i:]+cyc[:i]) for i in range(len(cyc))]
cyc = min(rots)
if cyc:
cycles.append(cyc)
else:
cycles.append(tuple())
# unique non-empty cycles
uniq = []
for c in cycles:
if c and c not in uniq:
uniq.append(c)
# basins: assign each start to its terminal cycle (if any)
for n in full_nodes:
visited = set()
x = n
seq = []
while x is not None and x not in visited:
visited.add(x)
seq.append(x)
x = T_ext.get(x, None)
cyc = tuple()
if x is not None and x in seq:
mu = seq.index(x)
cyc = tuple(seq[mu:])
if cyc:
rots = [tuple(cyc[i:]+cyc[:i]) for i in range(len(cyc))]
cyc = min(rots)
if cyc:
basins[cyc].append(n)
return uniq, basins, full_nodes, T_ext

cycles1, basins1, nodes1, T1_ext = cycles_and_basins_partial(T1)

# Inspect orbits starting from 0 under T0 (0-based) to compare with the user's "zero goes forever".
def iterate_until_repeat(T, seed, max_steps=200):
x = seed
seen = {}
path = []
for step in range(max_steps):
if x in seen:
return path, ("cycle_start_idx", seen[x]), ("cycle_period", step - seen[x])
seen[x] = step
path.append(x)
x = T.get(x, None)
if x is None:
return path, ("terminated", True), None
return path, ("max_steps_reached", True), None

# Also show orbits from 1..9 for T0 and T1 succinctly
def summarize_orbit(T, seed):
path, info, extra = iterate_until_repeat(T, seed, 100)
return {"seed": seed, "path": path, "info": info, "extra": extra}

C. What the 1-based correction changes conceptually
1. Two distinct dynamical layers
The π self-lookup layer
𝐹
𝜋
Fπ (1-based, decimal) is a single-sink system (absorbing fixed point at 1). This is your

The BBP layer
𝑇
T (hex, addressable) is a parity rotor whose cycles depend on the address base: the 0-based map
exhibits a 2-cycle and two fixed points; the 1-based shift reshapes those into a pair of fixed points and a 3-cycle.

from collections import defaultdict, deque
from typing import List, Dict, Tuple, Set
def digits(s: str) -> List[int]:
"""Return list of decimal digits from a string like '1415926…'."""
return [ord(c) - 48 for c in s if '0' <= c <= '9']
class PiRotor:
def __init__(self, pi_decimals: str):
# single source of truth: the digit tape you feed in
self.tape: List[int] = digits(pi_decimals) # 1-based semantics for f(n)=d_n
if not self.tape:
raise ValueError("Empty digit tape.")
self.N = len(self.tape)
def f(self, n: int) -> int:
"""1-based lookup: return d_n for n in [1..N]."""
if not (1 <= n <= self.N):
raise IndexError(f"n={n} outside 1..{self.N}")
return self.tape[n-1]
def orbit(self, seed: int, max_steps: int = 10_000) -> Dict[str, object]:
"""Iterate until a cycle is found or max_steps hit."""
path: List[int] = []
seen: Dict[int, int] = {} # node -> first index in path
n = seed
for step in range(max_steps):
path.append(n)
if n in seen:
# cycle detected starting at seen[n]
start = seen[n]
cycle = path[start:]
pre = path[:start]
return {"seed": seed, "pre": pre, "cycle": cycle}
seen[n] = len(path)-1
nxt = self.f(n)
# next index is the digit value; guard if 0 (undefined as next index)
if nxt == 0:
return {"seed": seed, "pre": path, "cycle": []}
n = nxt
return {"seed": seed, "pre": path, "cycle": []} # no close within bound
def adjacency(self) -> Dict[int, Set[int]]:
"""Directed edges i -> f(i) over valid indices (digits ≠ 0)."""
g: Dict[int, Set[int]] = defaultdict(set)
for i in range(1, self.N+1):
v = self.f(i)
if v != 0:
g[i].add(v)
return g
def parity_profile(self, seq: List[int]) -> List[str]:
"""Odd/Even label for a sequence of decimal digits (0..9)."""
lab = []
for x in seq:
if not (0 <= x <= 9):
lab.append("?")
else:
lab.append("E" if (x % 2 == 0) else "O")
return lab
# ----- Example usage -----
# supply ANY length you wish; here are the first 64 decimals (you can replace at will)
pi64 = "14159265358979323846264338327950288419716939937510582097494459"

# inspect orbits for seeds 1..9 (the nine “cannons”)
reports = [R.orbit(seed) for seed in range(1, 10)]
for rep in reports:
seed = rep["seed"]
pre, cyc = rep["pre"], rep["cycle"]
cyc_parity = R.parity_profile(cyc) if cyc else []
print(f"seed={seed:2d} pre={pre} cycle={cyc} parity={cyc_parity}")
# summarize image digits and “suppressed” digits in the window
image = sorted({R.f(i) for i in range(1, R.N+1)})

def cycle_signature(rep: Dict[str,object]) -> Tuple[int, ...]:

if not cyc: return ()

4) Why 1-based closes and 0-based wanders
In the 1-based definition, the graph you traverse is finite (nodes are 1..N), and edges are functional (each node has out-degree
1). Every such finite functional digraph decomposes into trees feeding cycles; closure is guaranteed somewhere.
In your 0-based variant you changed the node set and the successor rule; if the rule ever maps to 0 and you treat 0 specially (or
step outside 1..N), you no longer stay on a single finite functional component of the same form. The digraph you actually walk can
be much larger and need not revisit a small rotor quickly.
This is not numerology; it is graph theory: your “heartbeat” is the cycle in the functional graph induced by the given tape.

# Build the nine-cannon π self-lookup engine exactly as specified.
# Single source of truth: the first 64 digits of π after the decimal.
# No magic numbers: all horizons (history length, max ticks) derive from the
# tape length and discovered cycle length(s).
from collections import defaultdict, deque
from typing import List, Dict, Tuple, Set
import csv, json, os
from itertools import combinations
PI64 = (
"1415926535"
"8979323846"
"2643383279"
"5028841971"
"6939937510"
"5820974944"
"5923"
) # 64 digits
def digits(s: str) -> List[int]:
return [ord(c) - 48 for c in s if '0' <= c <= '9']
class PiRotor:
def __init__(self, pi_decimals: str):
self.tape: List[int] = digits(pi_decimals) # 1-based semantics for f(n)=d_n
if not self.tape:
raise ValueError("Empty digit tape.")
self.N = len(self.tape)
def f(self, n: int) -> int:
"""1-based lookup: return d_n for n in [1..N]."""
if not (1 <= n <= self.N):
raise IndexError(f"n={n} outside 1..{self.N}")
return self.tape[n-1]
def orbit(self, seed: int, max_steps: int = None) -> Dict[str, object]:
"""Iterate until a cycle is found or max_steps hit (default 2N)."""
if max_steps is None:
max_steps = 2*self.N # functional graph on N nodes => tail+cycle <= N, so 2N is safe
path: List[int] = []
pos: Dict[int, int] = {}
n = seed
for _ in range(max_steps):
path.append(n)
if n in pos:
start = pos[n]
cycle = path[start:]
pre = path[:start]
return {"seed": seed, "pre": pre, "cycle": cycle}
pos[n] = len(path)-1
nxt = self.f(n)
if nxt == 0: # undefined successor in 1-based domain
return {"seed": seed, "pre": path, "cycle": []}
n = nxt
return {"seed": seed, "pre": path, "cycle": []}
@staticmethod
def canonical_rotation(cyc: List[int]) -> Tuple[int, ...]:
if not cyc:
return ()
m = len(cyc)
rotations = [tuple(cyc[k:]+cyc[:k]) for k in range(m)]
return min(rotations)
def parity_label(self, x: int) -> str:
return "E" if (x % 2 == 0) else "O"
# Instantiate rotor
R = PiRotor(PI64)
# Step 1: discover cycles from seeds 1..9 (the nine "cannons")

reports = [R.orbit(seed) for seed in seed_range]

# Determine canonical rotor (choose the shortest discovered cycle; if tie, lexicographic min)
if unique_cycles:
lens = [len(c) for c in unique_cycles]

history = {i: [] for i in seed_range}

"unique_cycles": [list(c) for c in unique_cycles],

# Produce explicit seed → orbit traces for seeds 1..9 to include in the report.
from pprint import pprint
def orbit_trace(R, seed, limit=15):
seq = [seed]
n = seed
for _ in range(limit-1):
n = R.f(n)
seq.append(n)
return seq
traces = {i: orbit_trace(R, i, limit=10) for i in range(1,10)}
traces
